the reference representation used\n    66→  for every single-trial analysis is `obj.trialdat` = binned, causally smoothed firing rate.\n    67→- Cell quality filtering is a two-stage process: manual quality label, then a firing-rate\n    68→  threshold (`lowFR`). The paper states \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were\n    69→  included in all other analyses\" (1 Hz is also the value used in `WorkingWithDataObjs.m`).\n    70→- The reference code excludes photoinactivation (`stim.enable`) and early-lick (`early`)\n    71→  trials in every condition string it uses for ephys analyses.\n    72→\n    73→---\n    74→\n    75→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    76→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    77→\n    78→### Data Structure\n    79→`/app/data` contains four directories:\n    80→\n    81→| Directory | Contents | Used? |\n    82→|-----------|----------|-------|\n    83→| `Ephys_Behavior` | 25 `data_structure_*.mat` + 25 `motionEnergy_*.mat` — fixed-delay task sessions with ephys + video | **yes** |\n    84→| `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior` | 22 `data_structure_*.mat` + 22 `motionEnergy_*.mat` — randomized-delay task sessions with ephys + video | **yes** (19 of 22; see Step 4) |\n    85→| `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior` | behaviour + video only (optogenetic delay inhibition) | no — **no neural data** |\n    86→| `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior` | behaviour + video only (optogenetic go-cue inhibition) | no — **no neural data** |\n    87→\n    88→File formats: most `data_structure_*.mat` are MATLAB v7.3 (HDF5); 11 files\n    89→(`JEB23 2023-10-18/20/21`, all `JEB24`) are MATLAB v7 (MAT5). (from paper/methods)\n   132→| Statistic | Value | Source quote |\n   133→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   134→| Fixed-delay ephys dataset | 25 sessions, 9 mice, 1,651 units (483 single units) | \"For the DR task, we recorded 1,651 units (483 single units) in ALM from 25 sessions using nine mice.\" |\n   135→| Two-context subset | 12 sessions, 6 mice, 522 units (214 SU) | \"In 12 sessions from six mice, animals performed the two-context task. In total, 522 units (214 well-isolated single units) were recorded in these sessions.\" |\n   136→| Randomized-delay dataset | 19 sessions, 4 mice, 845 units (288 SU) | \"for the randomized delay task, we recorded 845 units (288 well-isolated single units) in ALM from 19 sessions using four mice\" |\n   137→| Session inclusion | ≥ 10 units | \"Recording sessions were included for analysis only if they had at least 10 units\" |\n   138→| Unit inclusion | FR > 1 Hz | \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included in all other analyses.\" |\n   139→| Neural time bin | 10 ms (`params.dt = 1/100`), 5 ms mentioned in the tutorial comment; decoding analyses re-bin to 75 ms | `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` |\n   140→| Alignment window | −2.5 … 2.5 s around the go cue | `params.tmin/tmax` |\n   141→| Video | 400 Hz, 2 cameras, DeepLabCut; missing values filled with nearest **except tongue** | Methods, \"Videography analysis\" |\n   142→| Motion energy | per frame, 99th percentile over pixels; all sessions start with DR | Methods |\n   147→| Early-lick trials | omitted from analyses | Methods |\n   148→| Photoinactivation | ~30 % of trials in opto sessions | Methods |\n   149→| Choice selectivity (DR) | sample 36 %, delay 42 %, response 58 % of 483 SU | Results |\n   150→| Context selectivity | 39 % of 214 SU (ITI) | Results |\n   151→\n   152→### Processing Details\n   153→- Temporal alignment for essentially all analyses is the **go cue** (`obj.bp.ev.goCue`); in\n   154→  WC trials the same field holds the water-drop time (paper axes read \"Time from go\n   155→  cue/water drop\").\n   156→- Neural data: spikes binned at `dt`, converted to spikes/s and causally smoothed\n   157→  (`mySmooth`, 15-bin gaussian, `reflect` boundary).\n   158→- Video: `frameTimes` are on the video clock; ALM Neuropixels/H2 recordings +\n  high-speed video during a two-context (delayed-response / water-cued) directional licking\n  task. Each session is a MATLAB struct `obj` with fields\n`bp` (bpod trial/task data), `clu` (sorted spikes, one cell per probe), `traj` (DeepLabCut\noutput, `{1}` = side cam, `{2}` = bottom cam), `sglx` (SpikeGLX metadata), `ex` (session\nmetadata incl. probe location), `me` (motion energy; only for behaviour-only sessions).\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `load<ANM>_ALMVideo` | DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/*.m | LOADING | **Definitive session + probe list**: which sessions have ephys+video and which probe is ALM. Commented-out entries = sessions the authors excluded. |\n| `loadObjs` | DataLoadingScripts/loadObjs.m | LOADING | loads `data_structure_<anm>_<date>.mat` for each entry of `meta` |\n| `loadSessionData` | DataLoadingScripts/loadSessionData.m | LOADING | per session/probe driver; concatenates units of both probes when `meta.probe = [1 2]` |\n| `processData` | DataLoadingScripts/processData.m | PROCESSING | findTrials → findClusters → alignSpikes → getSeq → removeLowFRClusters → baselineFR |\n| `findTrials` | DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m | CURATION | evaluates condition strings such as `'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'` over `obj.bp` fields |\n| `findClusters` | DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m | CURATION | with `params.quality={'all'}` keeps every cluster except quality `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, `real?` |\n| `alignSpikes` | DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m | PROCESSING | `trialtm_aligned = trialtm - ev.(alignEvent)(trial)`; `params.alignEvent='goCue'` |\n| `getSeq` | DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m | PROCESSING | histograms aligned spikes into `params.tmin:params.dt:params.tmax` bins, divides by `dt` (spikes/s) and smooths with `mySmooth`; produces `obj.trialdat (time, units, trials)` |\n| `mySmooth` | utils/mySmooth.m | PROCESSING | causal gaussian kernel: `gausswin(15)` with first 7 taps zeroed, normalised, `conv(...,'same')`, `'reflect'` boundary = prepend first 15 samples then trim |\n| `removeLowFRClusters` | DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m | CURATION | drops units whose mean firing rate over the window/trials is `<= params.lowFR` |\n| `getDefaultParams` / `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` | DataLoadingScripts | PROCESSING | parameters: `alignEvent='goCue'`, `tmin=-2.5`, `tmax=2.5`, `dt=1/100`, `smooth=15`, `bctype='reflect'`, `lowFR=1`, `quality={'all'}` |\n| `findVideoOffset` | funcs/findVideoOffset.m | PROCESSING | `vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs - mode(bp.ev.bitStart)`; the reference representation used\n  for every single-trial analysis is `obj.trialdat` = binned, causally smoothed firing rate.\n- Cell quality filtering is a two-stage process: manual quality label, then a firing-rate\n  threshold (`lowFR`). The paper states \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were\n  included in all other analyses\" (1 Hz is also the value used in `WorkingWithDataObjs.m`).\n- The reference code excludes photoinactivation (`stim.enable`) and early-lick (`early`)\n  trials in every condition string it uses for ephys analyses.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n`/app/data` contains four directories:\n\n| Directory | Contents | Used? |\n|-----------|----------|-------|\n| `Ephys_Behavior` | 25 `data_structure_*.mat` + 25 `motionEnergy_*.mat` — fixed-delay task sessions with ephys + video | **yes** |\n| `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior` | 22 `data_structure_*.mat` + 22 `motionEnergy_*.mat` — randomized-delay task sessions with ephys + video | **yes** (19 of 22; see Step 4) |\n| `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior` | behaviour + video only (optogenetic delay inhibition) | no — **no neural data** |\n| `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior` | behaviour + video only (optogenetic go-cue inhibition) | no — **no neural data** |\n\nFile formats: most `data_structure_*.mat` are MATLAB v7.3 (HDF5); 11 files\n(`JEB23 2023-10-18/20/21`, all `JEB24`) are MATLAB v7 (MAT5). This validates both the outcome\n   215→  and the lick-direction definitions.\n   216→- `numel(me.data) == Ntrials` and `numel(me.data{i}) == numel(frameTimes{i})` for all 44\n   217→  sessions → the motion-energy trace is sampled on the video frame clock.\n   218→- No session has `bp.fidx`, and spike `trial` indices always lie in `1..Ntrials`.\n   219→- `goCue` is finite on every trial of every session.\n   220→\n   221→---\n   222→\n   223→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   224→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   225→\n   226→### Variable Mapping\n   227→| Source variable | Target field | Transform | Reference function | Notes |\n   228→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|--------------------|-------|\n   229→| `clu{probe}(i).trialtm`, `.trial`, `bp.ev.goCue` | `neural[session][trial]` (n_units × 500) | align to go cue, 10 ms bins over [−2.5, 2.5] s, /dt, causal gaussian smoothing | `alignSpikes` + `getSeq` + `mySmooth` | float32 spikes/s |\n   230→| bin-centre times | `input[session][trial]` (1 × 500) | `edges + dt/2`, i.e. −2.495 … 2.495 s | `getSeq` (`obj.time`) | the only decoder input requested |\n   231→| `bp.R/L/hit/miss/no` | `output[0]` lick_direction | left=0, right=1, none=2 | `getPrevChoice` | per-trial, broadcast over time |\n   232→| `bp.autowater` | `output[1]` context | WC=0, DR=1 | `findTrials` condition strings | per-trial |\n   233→| `bp.hit/miss/no` | `output[2]` outcome | incorrect=0, correct=1, ignore=2 | `getOutcome` | per-trial |\n   234→| `traj{1}(t).ts[:, :, 'tongue']` | `output[3]` tongue_velocity | interpolate to the neural axis, speed = ‖d(x,y)/dbin‖, split at the session's 50th percentile; 2 where DLC did not track the tongue | `findPosition`, `findVelocity` | time-varying |\n   235→| `traj{2}(t).ts[:, :, 'top_paw']` | `output[4]` paw_velocity | as above | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `Scripts/Figure 1/Figure1e.m` (which uses `top_paw_yvel_view2` as \"paw speed\") | time-varying |\n   236→| `me.data{t}` | `output[5]` motion_energy | interpolate to the neural axis, split at the session's 50th percentile; 2 where no video frame covers the timepoint | `loadMotionEnergy` | time-varying |\n   237→| `ex.anm` / filename | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | | | 14 mice |\n   238→| `ex.probe(p).loc` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | `'* ALM'`→ALM, `'* M1TJ'`→tjM1, missing→ALM | | |\n   239→\n   240→### Key Decisions\n   241→1. **Which sessions**: all 44 ephys sessions listed in the reference `load*_ALMVideo.m`\n   242→   files (25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay). The two behaviour-only directories are\n   243→   excluded because they contain no neural data. Randomized-delay sessions are included\n   244→   because they are valid ALM recordings with the same task events, video and outcome\n   245→   variables; aligning on the go cue makes the variable delay irrelevant to the alignment\n   246→   (and the decoder is given time-from-go-cue as an input). They contribute DR-context\n   247→   trials only, which is correct, not degenerate.\n   248→2. **Which probe(s)**: exactly those in the reference load scripts (`meta.probe`), including\n   249→   the dual-probe JEB15 sessions where both probes are concatenated, as `loadSessionData`\n   250→   does.\n   251→3. **Alignment / window / binning**: go cue, [−2.5, 2.5] s, 10 ms bins — the reference\n   252→   `params` values. Every trial therefore has exactly 500 timepoints.\n   253→4. **Neural representation**: causally smoothed firing rate (spikes/s), exactly the\n   254→   `obj.trialdat` that all reference single-trial analyses (including their choice/context\n   255→   decoders) use. Smoothing is causal, so no information leaks backwards in time.\n   256→5. **Neuron curation**: quality label filter + mean FR > 1 Hz over the aligned window across\n   257→   **all** trials (reference: \"on average across all trials\"), computed before trial\n   258→   curation so the unit set does not depend on the trial subset.\n   259→6. **Trial curation**: drop `early` and `stim.enable` trials (as in every reference ephys\n   260→   condition string). Additionally drop trials with zero spikes on all units — in 2 sessions\n   261→   (JEB24 2023-10-23 and 2023-11-03) the ephys recording stopped before the behaviour did,\n   262→   leaving trailing trials with literally no neural data (they would be all-zero inputs and\n   263→   trigger the validator's \"all neural data is zero\" warning).\n   264→7. **Ignore trials are kept**: the decoder task explicitly asks for `none` lick direction and\n   265→   `ignore` outcome classes, so `bp.no` trials must be retained (the reference excludes them\n   266→   from its choice analyses, which is a task-specific difference required here).\n   267→8. **Discretisation**: per-session 50th percentile of all *valid* samples of the retained\n   268→   trials, exactly as instructed; class 2 = tongue/paw not tracked by DLC, or (motion\n   269→   energy) no video frame covering that timepoint. Per-session thresholds are necessary\n   270→   because camera/scale differences make the units incomparable across sessions (e.g.\n   271→   median tongue speed 3.8 px/bin in JEB6 vs 14 px/bin in JEB23).\n   272→9. **Tongue and paw features**: tongue = side-camera `tongue`; paw = bottom-camera\n   273→   `top_paw` (paws are only tracked from the bottom view, and `top_paw_yvel_view2` is the\n   274→   paw feature the authors use for \"paw speed\" in Fig. 1e). Speed is the magnitude of the\n   275→   2-D velocity, i.e. `sqrt(xvel² + yvel²)` of the reference's `xvel`/`yvel`.\n   276→10. **Velocity of a partially tracked feature**: the reference sets tongue velocity to 0\n   277→    wherever the position is NaN. Because \"not visible\" is a separate output class here, I\n   278→    instead compute the derivative with NaN-aware one-sided differences at the edges of each\n   279→    visibility bout, so that visible timepoints never get an artificial 0 (that would have\n   280→    mis-assigned ~30 % of visible tongue samples to the \"below median\" class).\n   281→11. **Per-trial outputs are emitted as time-varying rows** (constant within a trial) so that\n   282→    all six outputs share one `(6, 500)` array; the decoder broadcasts per-trial values the\n   283→    same way internally.\n   284→12. **Brain regions** are taken from `ex.probe.loc` rather than assumed, so the four JEB15\n   285→    sessions that include a tjM1 probe are labelled correctly.\n   286→\n   287→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   288→- [x] population PSTH shows the expected sharp increase at t = 0 (go cue) on lick trials and\n   289→      a much weaker one on ignore trials\n   290→- [x] lickport contacts occur only after t = 0, and their side matches `output[0]`\n   291→- [x] tongue is \"visible\" essentially only after the go cue\n   292→- [x] motion energy rises at the go cue\n   293→- [x] `hit+miss+no == Ntrials`, `R+L == Ntrials`\n   294→- [x] me trace length == number of video frames for every trial\n   295→- [ ] (Step 10) spot checks against the raw `.mat` files with `np.allclose`\n   296→- [ ] (Step 10) statistics vs the paper: sessions, mice, units, trials, performance\n   297→\n   298→---\n   299→\n   300→## Step 6: Script Development\n   301→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   302→\n   303→`/app/convert_data.py` (`python -u convert_data.py <out.pkl> [--full|--sample]\n   304→[--show-processing]`), structure:\n   305→\n   306→- `SESSIONS` — the 44 (animal, date, probes, directory, task) entries transcribed from the\n   307→  reference load scripts.\n   308→- `_H5Session` / `_V7Session` / `open_session` — a single reader interface over the two\n   309→  MATLAB file formats; only the arrays actually needed are read (for HDF5 files the DLC\n   310→  tensor is sliced per feature so a session's video data is never fully materialised).\n   311→- `load_motion_energy` — handles the three layouts found in the released\n   312→  `motionEnergy_*.mat` files (`me.data` cell array, `me.data.data` struct, bare cell array).\n   313→- `gausswin`, `_causal_kernel`, `my_smooth` — exact ports of `mySmooth.m`; since the kernel's\n   314→  first `N//2` taps are zero, `conv(...,'same')` is equivalent to a causal FIR filter, which\n   315→  is implemented with `scipy.signal.lfilter` (vectorised over all units × trials at once).\n   316→- `bin_spikes` — vectorised port of `alignSpikes` + `getSeq`: one `np.bincount` over\n   317→  `(trial, bin)` per unit instead of a MATLAB double loop with `histc`.\n   318→- `mean_firing_rates` / low-FR removal — port of `removeLowFRClusters`.\n   319→- `kinematic_speed`, `motion_energy_trace` — ports of `findPosition`/`findVelocity` and\n   320→  `loadMotionEnergy` (`interp_matlab` reproduces MATLAB `interp1`: NaN outside the data\n   321→  range and NaN propagated from NaN samples).\n   322→- `discretize` — 50th-percentile split with the third \"invalid\" class.\n   323→- `_plot_processing` — the `--show-processing` figure (8 panels: spike raster/rate with lick\n   324→  times and task events, population PSTH by lick direction, and traces + distributions with\n   325→  the median threshold marked for each of the three continuous variables).\n   326→\n   327→Code inefficiencies identified and removed:\n   328→- per-spike Python loops → `np.bincount` on a flattened (trial, bin) index;\n   329→- per-unit/per-trial smoothing → one `lfilter` call over a `(500, units·trials)` matrix;\n   330→- reading the whole `(nfeat, 3, nframes)` DLC tensor per trial → HDF5 hyperslab of the two\n   331→  needed rows;\n   332→- `lfilter` returns float64 → cast back to float32 (halves the pickle size).\n   333→\n   334→Run time: **~4 s per session** (≈2.3 s of which is video interpolation), so the full\n   335→44-session conversion is ≈3 minutes — well under the 15-minute budget, no parallelism needed.\n   336→\n   337→---\n 518→### Check 3 — reference code comparison\n   519→| Stage | Reference | `convert_data.py` | Same? |\n   520→|-------|-----------|-------------------|-------|\n   521→| (a) data loading | yes. Additional: trials with no ephys coverage (not in the reference, justified in Step 5). Ignore trials are kept (required by the task's `none`/`ignore` output classes). |\n   524→| (d) temporal alignment | `alignSpikes.m`: `trialtm - ev.goCue(trial)`; video: `frameTimes - findVideoOffset(obj) - ev.goCue(trial)`; motion energy identical | identical formulas | yes |\n   525→| (e) binning | `getSeq.m`: `histc` into `tmin:dt:tmax`, drop the last bin, `/dt`, `mySmooth(...,15,'reflect')`; `obj.time = edges + dt/2` | `bin_spikes` + `my_smooth`; time axis identical | yes (verified numerically in Check 2) |\n   526→| (f) input construction | (no analogue — the reference has no decoder input beyond neural data) | bin-centre time, as required by the task | task requirement |\n   527→| (g) output construction | `getPrevChoice.m` (choice), `getOutcome.m` (outcome), `autowater` (context), `findPosition/findVelocity` (kinematics), `loadMotionEnergy` (ME) | same definitions | yes, with two deliberate differences (below) |\n   528→\n   529→Deliberate differences from the reference, and why:\n   530→1. **Ignore (`bp.no`) trials are kept.** Every reference ephys condition string selects\n   531→   `hit`/`miss` only, but the decoder task requires `none` (lick direction) and `ignore`\n   532→   (outcome) classes, so these trials must be present.\n   533→2. **Tongue velocity at the edges of a visibility bout.** `findVelocity.m` replaces NaN\n   534→   tongue velocities by 0; here \"not visible\" is its own output class, so a NaN-aware\n   535→   derivative (one-sided at bout edges) is used instead. Using the reference's rule would\n   536→   have assigned an artificial speed of 0 — hence class \"below median\" — to ~30 % of the\n   537→   timepoints at which the tongue *is* visible.\n   538→3. **Velocity magnitude** `sqrt(xvel² + yvel²)` rather than the reference's separate\n   539→   `xvel`/`yvel` features, because the task asks for a single \"velocity\" variable to\n   540→   discretise. The reference's per-axis baseline subtraction (`- basederiv(1)`, a constant\n   541→   ≈ 0 that `findVelocity.m` applies to both axes) is omitted; it shifts speeds by < 0.01\n   542→   px/bin and cannot change a median split materially.\n   543→4. **Case-insensitive quality labels** (the reference's `ismember` is case-sensitive and\n   544→   would keep the single `Noisy`-labelled unit in JEB6).\n   545→5. **Median (50th-percentile) thresholds** for the three continuous variables instead of\n   546→   the authors' manual `me.moveThresh`: required explicitly by the decoder-task\n   547→   specification. (For reference, `me.moveThresh` corresponds to the 43rd–88th percentile\n   548→   depending on the session, mean ≈ 65th.)\n   549→\n   550→### Check 4 — key statistics comparison (`/app/cache/paper_stats.py`)\n   551→Everything in the Step 9 table above was checked. 2 where no video frame covers the timepoint | `loadMotionEnergy` | time-varying |\n| `ex.anm` / filename | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | | | 14 mice |\n| `ex.probe(p).loc` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | `'* ALM'`→ALM, `'* M1TJ'`→tjM1, missing→ALM | | |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Which sessions**: all 44 ephys sessions listed in the reference `load*_ALMVideo.m`\n   files (25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay). The two behaviour-only directories are\n   excluded because they contain no neural data. Randomized-delay sessions are included\n   because they are valid ALM recordings with the same task events, video and outcome\n   variables; aligning on the go cue makes the variable delay irrelevant to the alignment\n   (and the decoder is given time-from-go-cue as an input). They contribute DR-context\n   trials only, which is correct, not degenerate.\n2. **Which probe(s)**: exactly those in the reference load scripts (`meta.probe`), including\n   the dual-probe JEB15 sessions where both probes are concatenated, as `loadSessionData`\n   does.\n3. **Alignment / window / binning**: go cue, [−2.5, 2.5] s, 10 ms bins — the reference\n   `params` values. Every trial therefore has exactly 500 timepoints.\n4. **Neural representation**: causally smoothed firing rate (spikes/s), exactly the\n   `obj.trialdat` that all reference single-trial analyses (including their choice/context\n   decoders) use. Smoothing is causal, so no information leaks backwards in time.\n5. **Neuron curation**: quality label filter + mean FR > 1 Hz over the aligned window across\n   **all** trials (reference: \"on average across all trials\"), computed before trial\n   curation so the unit set does not depend on the trial subset.\n6. **Trial curation**: drop `early` and `stim.enable` trials (as in every reference ephys\n   condition string). Additionally drop trials with zero spikes on all units — in 2 sessions\n   (JEB24 2023-10-23 and 2023-11-03) the ephys recording stopped before the behaviour did,\n   leaving trailing trials with literally no neural data (they would be all-zero inputs and\n   trigger the validator's \"all neural data is zero\" warning).\n7. **Ignore trials are kept**: the decoder task explicitly asks for `none` lick direction and\n   `ignore` outcome classes, so `bp.no` trials must be retained (the reference excludes them\n   from its choice analyses, which is a task-specific difference required here).\n8. **Discretisation**: per-session 50th percentile of all *valid* samples of the retained\n   trials, exactly as instructed; class 2 = tongue/paw not tracked by DLC, or (motion\n   energy) no video frame covering that timepoint. Per-session thresholds are necessary\n   because camera/scale differences make the units incomparable across sessions (e.g.\n   median tongue speed 3.8 px/bin in JEB6 vs 14 px/bin in JEB23).\n9. **Tongue and paw features**: tongue = side-camera `tongue`; paw = bottom-camera\n   `top_paw` (paws are only tracked from the bottom view, and `top_paw_yvel_view2` is the\n   paw feature the authors use for \"paw speed\" in Fig. 1e). Speed is the magnitude of the\n   2-D velocity, i.e. `sqrt(xvel² + yvel²)` of the reference's `xvel`/`yvel`.\n10. **Velocity of a partially tracked feature**: the reference sets tongue velocity to 0\n    wherever the position is NaN. Because \"not visible\" is a separate output class here, I\n    instead compute the derivative with NaN-aware one-sided differences at the edges of each\n    visibility bout, so that visible timepoints never get an artificial 0 (that would have\n    mis-assigned ~30 % of visible tongue samples to the \"below median\" class).\n11. **Per-trial outputs are emitted as time-varying rows** (constant within a trial) so that\n    all six outputs share one `(6, 500)` array; the decoder broadcasts per-trial values the\n    same way internally.\n12. **Brain regions** are taken from `ex.probe.loc` rather than assumed, so the four JEB15\n    sessions that include a tjM1 probe are labelled correctly.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] population PSTH shows the expected sharp increase at t = 0 (go cue) on lick trials and\n      a much weaker one on ignore trials\n- [x] lickport contacts occur only after t = 0, and their side matches `output[0]`\n- [x] tongue is \"visible\" essentially only after the go cue\n- [x] motion energy rises at the go cue\n- [x] `hit+miss+no == Ntrials`, `R+L == Ntrials`\n- [x] me trace length == number of video frames for every trial\n- [ ] (Step 10) spot checks against the raw `.mat` files with `np.allclose`\n- [ ] (Step 10) statistics vs the paper: sessions, mice, units, trials, performance\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` (`python -u convert_data.py <out.pkl> [--full|--sample]\n[--show-processing]`), structure:\n\n- `SESSIONS` — the 44 (animal, date, probes, directory, task) entries transcribed from the\n  reference load scripts.\n- `_H5Session` / `_V7Session` / `open_session` — a single reader interface over the two\n  MATLAB file formats; only the arrays actually needed are read (for HDF5 files the DLC\n  tensor is sliced per feature so a session's video data is never fully materialised).\n- `load_motion_energy` — handles the three layouts found in the released\n  `motionEnergy_*.mat` files (`me.data` cell array, `me.data.data` struct, bare cell array).\n- `gausswin`, `_causal_kernel`, `my_smooth` — exact ports of `mySmooth.m`; since the kernel's\n  first `N//2` taps are zero, `conv(...,'same')` is equivalent to a causal FIR filter, which\n  is implemented with `scipy.signal.lfilter` (vectorised over all units × trials at once).\n- `bin_spikes` — vectorised port of `alignSpikes` + `getSeq`: one `np.bincount` over\n  `(trial, bin)` per unit instead of a MATLAB double loop with `histc`.\n- `mean_firing_rates` / low-FR removal — port of `removeLowFRClusters`.\n- `kinematic_speed`, `motion_energy_trace` — ports of `findPosition`/`findVelocity` and\n  `loadMotionEnergy` (`interp_matlab` reproduces MATLAB `interp1`: NaN outside the data\n  range and NaN propagated from NaN samples).\n- `discretize` — 50th-percentile split with the third \"invalid\" class.\n- `_plot_processing` — the `--show-processing` figure (8 panels: spike raster/rate with lick\n  times and task events, population PSTH by lick direction, and traces + distributions with\n  the median threshold marked for each of the three continuous variables).\n\nCode inefficiencies identified and removed:\n- per-spike Python loops → `np.bincount` on a flattened (trial, bin) index;\n- per-unit/per-trial smoothing → one `lfilter` call over a `(500, units·trials)` matrix;\n- reading the whole `(nfeat, 3, nframes)` DLC tensor per trial → HDF5 hyperslab of the two\n  needed rows;\n- `lfilter` returns float64 → cast back to float32 (halves the pickle size).\n\nRun time: **~4 s per session** (≈2.3 s of which is video interpolation), so the full\n44-session conversion is ≈3 minutes — well under the 15-minute budget, no parallelism needed.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n→ `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`, smooth with an independent implementation of `mySmooth`, compare to the\n   pickle. `np.allclose(..., atol=1e-4)` passes with max |difference| ≈ 3e-6 (float32\n   rounding).\n2. **Neural bookkeeping** — `n_neurons` of every trial equals the session's unit count and\n   the length of `brain_region_idx`, for all 44 sessions.\n3. **Input** — equals the bin centres `-2.495 … 2.495` for every checked trial of every\n   session.\n4. **Outputs, per-trial** — `lick_direction`, `context` and `outcome` recomputed from the\n   raw `bp.R/L/hit/miss/no/autowater` fields match for **all** trials of 5 comparison\n| Stage | Reference | `convert_data.py` | Same? |\n|-------|-----------|-------------------|-------|\n| (a) data loading | `loadObjs.m` + `load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m` session/probe lists; `loadMotionEnergy.m` | `SESSIONS` transcribed from those files; `open_session`, `load_motion_energy` | yes (also handles the v7 files, which the MATLAB code reads transparently) |\n| (b) neuron filtering | `findClusters.m` (`quality={'all'}` → drop garbage/gabrga/noisy/real?) then `removeLowFRClusters.m` (`lowFR=1`) | identical two stages | yes, except the label match is case-insensitive (Step 4) |\n| (c) trial filtering | condition strings `'...&~stim.enable&~early'` in `findTrials.m` | `early < .5 & stim.enable < .5` | yes. Additional: trials with no ephys coverage (not in the reference, justified in Step 5). Ignore trials are kept (required by the task's `none`/`ignore` output classes). |\n| (d) temporal alignment | `alignSpikes.m`: `trialtm - ev.goCue(trial)`; video: `frameTimes - findVideoOffset(obj) - ev.goCue(trial)`; motion energy identical | identical formulas | yes |\n| (e) binning | `getSeq.m`: `histc` into `tmin:dt:tmax`, drop the last bin, `/dt`, `mySmooth(...,15,'reflect')`; `obj.time = edges + dt/2` | `bin_spikes` + `my_smooth`; time axis identical | yes (verified numerically in Check 2) |\n| (f) input construction | (no analogue — the reference has no decoder input beyond neural data) | bin-centre time, as required by the task | task requirement |\n| (g) output construction | `getPrevChoice.m` (choice), `getOutcome.m` (outcome), `autowater` (context), `findPosition/findVelocity` (kinematics), `loadMotionEnergy` (ME) | same definitions | yes, with two deliberate differences (below) |\n\nDeliberate differences from the reference, and why:\n1. **Ignore (`bp.no`) trials are kept.** Every reference ephys condition string selects\n   `hit`/`miss` only, but the decoder task requires `none` (lick direction) and `ignore`\n   (outcome) classes, so these trials must be present.\n2. **Tongue velocity at the edges of a visibility bout.** `findVelocity.m` replaces NaN\n   tongue velocities by 0; here \"not visible\" is its own output class, so a NaN-aware\n   derivative (one-sided at bout edges) is used instead. Using the reference's rule would\n   have assigned an artificial speed of 0 — hence class \"below median\" — to ~30 % of the\n   timepoints at which the tongue *is* visible.\n3. **Velocity magnitude** `sqrt(xvel² + yvel²)` rather than the reference's separate\n   `xvel`/`yvel` features, because the task asks for a single \"velocity\" variable to\n   discretise. The reference's per-axis baseline subtraction (`- basederiv(1)`, a constant\n   ≈ 0 that `findVelocity.m` applies to both axes) is omitted; it shifts speeds by < 0.01\n   px/bin and cannot change a median split materially.\n4. **Case-insensitive quality labels** (the reference's `ismember` is case-sensitive and\n   would keep the single `Noisy`-labelled unit in JEB6).\n5. **Median (50th-percentile) thresholds** for the three continuous variables instead of\n   the authors' manual `me.moveThresh`: required explicitly by the decoder-task\n   specification. (For reference, `me.moveThresh` corresponds to the 43rd–88th percentile\n   depending on the session, mean ≈ 65th.)\n\n### Check 4 — key statistics comparison (`/app/cache/paper_stats.py`)\nEverything in the Step 9 table above was checked. The two genuine mismatches:\n\n- **Mice in the fixed-delay dataset**: paper says nine, but the authors' own load scripts\n  and the released files both contain ten (EKH1, EKH3, JEB6, JEB7, JEB13, JEB14, JEB15,\n  JEB19, JGR2, JGR3) for exactly the 25 sessions the paper reports. Code+data agree with\n  each other, so the paper's count appears to be an error; I follow code+data.\n- **Unit counts**: paper 1,651 / 845 (483 / 288 single units); mine 1,532 / 925\n  (270 / 173). I investigated this at length:\n  - the quality-curated counts before the 1 Hz filter are 1,565 / 948, so the FR filter is\n    not the source of the difference;\n  - no combination of quality labels reproduces 483/288 single units (excellent+great+good\n    = 282/178, adding `fair` = 647/444), so the paper's single-unit criterion (manual ISI\n    inspection) is not recoverable from the released `quality` strings;\n  - restricting to the ALM probe only, or including both probes of the dual-probe sessions,\n    moves the totals further away;\n  - the paper gives no per-session table (Extended Data Fig. 2a is a bar plot), so the\n    difference cannot be localised to specific sessions.\n  I therefore keep the curation the reference **code** specifies (which is unambiguous and\n  reproducible) and document the ±5–10 % discrepancy with the paper's reported totals.\n- The paper's \"12 sessions, six mice, 522 units\" two-context subset could not be reproduced\n  either: applying the paper's behavioural criterion (≥ 40 correct DR trials per direction\n  and ≥ 20 correct WC trials per direction) selects 7 sessions/5 mice, while merely\n  requiring ≥ 20 WC trials per direction selects 9. This does not affect the conversion,\n  because context is labelled per trial and all sessions are kept.\n\n### Check 5 — edge cases (`/app/cache/edge_cases.py`)\n- All 13,762 trials have exactly 500 timepoints; neural float32, input float32, output int8;\n  `subject_idx` int64 in [0, 13]; all `brain_region_idx` int64 with valid indices.\n- Smallest session: 193 trials (≥ 2 required); smallest unit count 17 (≥ 10 required).\n- Bin-edge handling matches MATLAB `histc` + `N(1:end-1)`: a spike exactly at t = −2.5 falls\n  in the first bin and one exactly at t = +2.5 is dropped.\n- No silent or constant units within the retained trials. Two of the 2,457 units fall just\n  below 1 Hz *when recomputed on the retained trials only* (0.988 and 0.999 Hz) because the\n  reference computes the criterion over **all** trials; this is the reference behaviour and\n  is left as is.\n- Partial ephys coverage: only 6 trials (in 3 sessions) have no spikes in the last 0.5 s of\n  the window; complete-coverage failures (0 spikes anywhere in the trial) were removed by\n  the \"no ephys\" filter (64 trials, JEB24 2023-10-23 and 2023-11-03).\n- 12 of the 44 sessions contain only DR trials, so `context` is constant within them; this\n  is a property of the experiment, not a bug, and the decoder is trained across sessions.\n  One session (JEB24 2023-10-23) has no ignore trials after curation.\n- The 14 trials (0.09 %) where `lick_direction` disagrees with the \"first lick\" heuristic\n  were traced to two harmless causes: (i) `bp.no` (ignore) trials on which the animal\n  contacted a port 3.9–6.0 s after the go cue, i.e. outside the 3 s response window (and\n  outside our analysis window), and (ii) trials where the same contact time is registered on\n  **both** lick channels (sensor cross-talk), for which the heuristic has no defined answer.\n  The bpod scoring fields used for the conversion are correct in both cases.\n\n### Issues found and resolved\n| Issue | Resolution |\n|-------|------------|\n| 11 of the 47 data objects are MATLAB v7, not v7.3 | added a second reader; both paths verified by the Check-2 spot tests |\n| 3 layouts of `motionEnergy_*.mat` (`me.data` cell, `me.data.data` struct, bare cell array) | all handled in `load_motion_energy`, mirroring `loadMotionEnergy.m` |\n| `data_structure_JEB24_2023-10-03.mat` has no `clu` field | it is not in the reference session list; excluded |\n| `JEB23 2023-10-20` duplicates `2023-10-19` | not in the reference session list; excluded |\n| 64 trailing trials in 2 sessions have no spikes at all | excluded (would be all-zero neural inputs) |\n| smoothed output was float64 | cast to float32 (pickle 3.3 → 1.64 GB) |\n| the first cross-check of `lick_direction` failed on 2 sessions | root-caused (late licks on ignore trials; simultaneous contacts on both channels); the check was refined to use the 3 s response window and a tie rule, and now passes |\n\n---\n\n## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n→ `/app/train_decoder_full_out.txt` (≈3 min on GPU), plus `sample_trials.png` and\n`predictions.png`.\n\n### Training Progress\n- Loss decreasing: **yes**, monotonically from 4.00 (epoch 1) → 0.7286 (epoch 200);\n  test loss 0.7684 (close to the training loss).\n- Trained on 11,001 trials, validated on 2,761 held-out trials (every session contributes to\n  both).\n\n### Decoder Results (Full, 44 sessions)\n| Output | Training balanced acc | Validation balanced acc | Chance | Val / chance |\n|--------|----------------------|-------------------------|--------|--------------|\n| lick_direction | 0.651 | 0.619 | 0.333 | 1.86× |\n| context | 0.859 | 0.860 | 0.500 | 1.72× |\n| outcome | 0.639 | 0.608 | 0.333 | 1.83× |\n| tongue_velocity | 0.584 | 0.572 | 0.333 | 1.72× |\n| paw_velocity | 0.549 | 0.543 | 0.333 | 1.63× |\n| motion_energy | 0.720 | 0.716 | 0.333 | 2.15× |\n\n---\n\n## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Check 1 — accuracy vs chance\n| Output | Validation balanced acc | Chance (1/k) | Ratio | Majority-class rate |\n|--------|------------------------|--------------|-------|---------------------|\n| lick_direction | 0.619 | 0.333 | 1.86× | 0.446 |\n| context | 0.860 | 0.500 | 1.72× | 0.903 |\n| outcome | 0.608 | 0.333 | 1.83× | 0.749 |\n| tongue_velocity | 0.572 | 0.333 | 1.72× | 0.909 |\n| paw_velocity | 0.543 | 0.333 | 1.63× | 0.403 |\n| motion_energy | 0.716 | 0.333 | 2.15× | 0.481 |\n\nNo output is at or below chance; all are ≥ 1.6× chance. `paw_velocity` is the weakest, which\nis expected: forepaw movements are uninstructed, and 19 % of its samples are the\n\"not visible\" class, a mixture of DeepLabCut drop-out and window edges that ALM activity\ncannot be expected to predict.\n\n### Check 2 — comparison with the accuracies reported in the paper\nThe paper's decoding numbers (Fig. 3b, Fig. 4b, ED Fig. 2c) come from a **different\nestimator**: one linear SVM **per session and per 75 ms time bin**, trained on balanced\ncorrect left/right (or DR/WC) trials, 4-fold CV. The provided `train_decoder.py` instead\nfits **one** linear readout shared by all 44 sessions and all 500 timepoints, on all trial\ntypes. To compare like with like, I re-ran the paper's analysis on my converted data\n(`/app/cache/reproduce_fig3b_4b.py`, figure `/app/cache/reproduce_fig3b_4b.png`),\nfollowing `NeuralChoiceDecoding.m` / `NeuralContextDecoding.m` / `DLC_ChoiceDecoder.m`\n(min-max normalisation to [−1, 1], 75 ms bins, equal class counts, 4-fold CV, LinearSVC):\n\n| Epoch | Choice (L vs R), this conversion | Choice, paper (Fig. 3b) | Context, this conversion | Context, paper (Fig. 4b) |\n|-------|----------------------------------|-------------------------|--------------------------|--------------------------|\n| ITI (−2.5…−2.0 s) | 0.544 | ≈ 0.5–0.55 | 0.792 | ≈ 0.75–0.85 |\n| sample (−2.2…−1.0 s) | 0.690 | rises from ≈0.55 | 0.825 | ≈ 0.8 |\n| late delay (−0.6…0 s) | 0.846 | ≈ 0.8–0.85 | 0.833 | ≈ 0.8–0.85 |\n| response (0.1…1.0 s) | 0.922 | ≈ 0.9–0.95 | 0.854 | ≈ 0.85–0.9 |\n| peak | **0.953** | **≈ 0.95–1.0** | **0.926** | **≈ 0.9–0.95** |\n| shuffled labels | 0.504 | ≈ 0.5 | 0.503 | ≈ 0.5 |\n\nThe reproduced time courses are essentially identical to the published figures: choice\ndecoding is at chance during the ITI, **jumps exactly at the sample-tone onset (−2.2 s)**,\ngrows through the delay, peaks just after the go cue and then decays; context decoding is\nhigh (≈ 0.8) throughout the trial including the ITI, with steps at the sample onset and the go\ncue. The sharp inflection at −2.2 s (= −1.3 s sample − 0.9 s delay) is an independent\nconfirmation that the temporal alignment is correct to within a bin. The paper's\nED Fig. 2c AUC of 0.86 ± 0.11 for delay-epoch choice decoding likewise matches my late-delay\naccuracy of 0.846.\n\n**Conclusion**: the converted data supports exactly the decoding accuracies the paper\nreports. The lower numbers from `train_decoder.py` are a property of its shared-readout,\nall-timepoints-at-once architecture, not of the conversion. Two further controls support\nthis:\n- a *per-session* linear SVM restricted to one model for all timepoints (i.e. the same\n  framing as the provided decoder but with no cross-session sharing and no time input)\n  is **worse** than the provided decoder on every output (lick_direction 0.631 vs 0.619\n  with far fewer training samples per model, tongue 0.522 vs 0.572, paw 0.473 vs 0.543,\n  motion energy 0.663 vs 0.716) — `/app/cache/per_session_kinematics.py`;\n- the time-resolved accuracy of the provided decoder (`/app/cache/time_resolved_accuracy.py`)\n  has the same shape as the paper's curves (left-vs-right 0.46 in the ITI → 0.63 in the late\n  delay → 0.73 in the response epoch; context flat at ≈0.85), i.e. the information is where\n  the paper says it is, just read out by a weaker model.\n\n### Check 3 — train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Validation | Train/Val |\n|--------|-------|-----------|-----------|\n| lick_direction | 0.651 | 0.619 | 1.05 |\n| context | 0.859 | 0.860 | 1.00 |\n| outcome | 0.639 | 0.608 | 1.05 |\n| tongue_velocity | 0.584 | 0.572 | 1.02 |\n| paw_velocity | 0.549 | 0.543 | 1.01 |\n| motion_energy | 0.720 | 0.716 | 1.01 |\n\nNo gap exceeds 1.05×, so there is no overfitting and no data leakage (the split is by trial,\nand no quantity used to build a trial is computed from other trials' *labels*; the only\nsession-level quantities are the three median thresholds and the unit-selection firing\nrates, which are label-independent).\n\n### Additional experiments run in this review\n1. **Neural normalisation** (`/app/cache/test_normalization.py`, 11-session subset). The\n   reference decoding code min-max normalises the firing rates per session; I tested raw\n   rates, per-neuron z-scoring, per-session min-max and √-transform:\n   raw 0.593/0.813/0.579/0.564/0.540/0.732, z-score 0.594/0.784/0.568/0.582/0.467/0.733,\n   min-max 0.535/0.797/0.568/0.536/0.521/0.693, √ 0.603/0.821/0.588/0.577/0.553/0.739.\n   Raw smoothed firing rates (what `obj.trialdat` contains) are as good as any alternative,\n   so the dataset ships un-normalised rates — the representation the reference produces, and\n   the one a downstream user can transform however they like.\n2. **Time-bin size** (`/app/cache/test_binsize.py`, same subset): accuracy increases\n   monotonically with bin width (lick_direction 0.593 at 10 ms, 0.596 at 20 ms, 0.618 at\n   50 ms, 0.636 at 100 ms, 0.661 at 200 ms), because averaging bins denoises the per-timepoint\n   neural vector — there is no optimum short of destroying the time resolution of the\n   outputs. I therefore kept the reference's `params.dt = 10 ms` rather than chasing\n   accuracy: it is the binning the reference pipeline uses to build `obj.trialdat`, it\n   preserves the ~40 ms structure of the tongue-visibility bouts that two of the outputs\n   encode, and a user who wants the reference's *decoding* representation can average 7–8\n   bins (as `DLC_ChoiceDecoder.m` does, and as my Fig. 3b reproduction above does).\n\n### Issues found and resolved in this step\n- None that required changing the conversion. How are **all the data** for all subjects, sessions, and trials loaded in?\n\ni. Each session is one MATLAB `.mat` file (`data_structure_<anm>_<date>.mat`) located in either `Ephys_Behavior` or `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior`. The 44 sessions and their probes are hardcoded in a `SESSIONS` list, transcribed from the authors' `load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m` scripts. Two file format readers are implemented (`_H5Session` for v7.3/HDF5 and `_V7Session` for v7/MAT5) since the data files come in both formats. Motion energy is loaded separately from `motionEnergy_<anm>_<date>.mat` files.\n\nii.\n```python\nSESSIONS = [\n    # (animal, date, probes (1-based), data directory, task)\n    ('EKH1',  '2021-08-07', [2],    DATA_FIXED, 'DR/WC fixed delay'),\n    ...\n    ('JEB24', '2023-11-03', [1],    DATA_RAND,  'DR randomized delay'),\n]\n\ndef open_session(path):\n    return _H5Session(path) if _is_hdf5(path) else _V7Session(path)\n```\n\niii. The session list follows the authors' own loading scripts exactly, including which sessions are commented out. Both MATLAB formats are handled because 11 of the 47 data files are v7 rather than v7.3.\n\n## 1-b. How are the data split into subjects?\n\ni. The animal name is the first element of each SESSIONS tuple (e.g., `'EKH1'`). A list of unique subjects is built during assembly by appending new animal names as they appear. `subject_idx` maps each session to its subject index.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor k, (anm, date, probes, datadir, task) in enumerate(sessions):\n    ...\n    if anm not in subjects:\n        subjects.append(anm)\n    ...\n    data['subject_idx'].append(subjects.index(anm))\n```\n\niii. The animal name is taken from the session metadata tuple, which was transcribed from the reference loading scripts. The 44 sessions span 14 animals.\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. One session is one entry in the `SESSIONS` list, keyed by `(animal, date, probes, datadir, task)`. The `process_session` function loads and converts one session at a time. Fixed-delay (25) and randomized-delay (19) sessions are processed uniformly.\n\nii.\n```python\nfn = os.path.join(datadir, f'data_structure_{anm}_{date}.mat')\nobj = open_session(fn)\n```\n\niii. Sessions follow the reference `load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m` scripts exactly, including the probe assignments.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials are defined by the Bpod trial table (`obj.bp`). The number of trials is `obj.bp.Ntrials`. Each per-trial field (hit, miss, R, L, etc.) has `Ntrials` entries. Spike times carry per-spike trial indices (`clu.trial`), so trial boundaries don't need to be reconstructed.\n\nii.\n```python\nntrials = obj.Ntrials  # int(np.array(self.o['bp']['Ntrials']).ravel()[0])\ngocue = obj.bp('ev.goCue')\nhit, miss, no = obj.bp('hit'), obj.bp('miss'), obj.bp('no')\n```\n\niii. The Bpod table directly defines trials with one go cue per trial. No inference of trial boundaries is needed.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Three filters are applied: (1) Early-lick trials (`bp.early`) are excluded; (2) Photostimulation trials (`bp.stim.enable`) are excluded; (3) Trials with no spikes on any unit (ephys recording ended before the behavioral session) are excluded. Additionally, trials with non-finite go cue times are excluded. Across the dataset, 13,762 of ~15,000 trials are retained.\n\nii.\n```python\nkeep = (early < 0.5) & (stim < 0.5) & np.isfinite(gocue)\nspk_per_trial = trialdat.sum(axis=(0, 1))\nno_ephys = spk_per_trial <= 0\nkeep &= ~no_ephys\n```\n\niii. Early-lick and photostim removal follows the reference condition strings (`~stim.enable & ~early`). The no-ephys filter addresses sessions where the recording stopped before the behavioral session ended (64 trials in 2 sessions).\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. `clu{probe}(i).trialtm` (spike times relative to trial start), `clu{probe}(i).trial` (trial index per spike, 1-based), and `bp.ev.goCue` (go cue times for alignment). Quality labels `clu{probe}(i).quality` are used for filtering.\n\nii.\n```python\ntt, tr = obj.spikes(prb, i)  # trialtm, trial\nal = tt - gocue[tr - 1]      # trialtm_aligned\n```\n\niii. These are the same variables used by the reference's `alignSpikes.m`.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Aligned spike times are binned into 10 ms non-overlapping bins over [-2.5, 2.5] s (500 bins), divided by the bin width to get spikes/s, then smoothed with a causal Gaussian kernel ported from `mySmooth.m`: a `gausswin(15)` with first 7 taps zeroed and normalized, applied as a causal FIR filter using `scipy.signal.lfilter`. The boundary condition is 'reflect' (prepend reflected samples). Units from both probes of dual-probe sessions are concatenated.\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 0.01  # params.dt = 1/100 s  -> 10 ms bins\nSMOOTH_N = 15\nEDGES, TAXIS = time_axis()  # edges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT/2, DT)\n\ndef _causal_kernel(N=SMOOTH_N):\n    k = gausswin(N)\n    k[:N // 2] = 0.0\n    k = k / k.sum()\n    return k[N // 2:]\n\nout /= DT  # spikes / s\nsm = my_smooth(out.reshape(NT, -1)).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The AI explicitly ports `mySmooth.m` and uses `params.dt = 1/100` (10 ms) from `getDefaultParams.m`/`WorkingWithDataObjs.m`. The AI notes that 5 ms is mentioned in a tutorial comment but chose 10 ms as the main parameter value.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two-stage filtering: (1) Manual quality labels are checked case-insensitively against `{garbage, gabrga, noisy, real?}` — clusters with these labels are dropped. (2) Units whose mean firing rate over the analysis window across all trials is <= 1 Hz are dropped. This yields 2,457 units across 44 sessions.\n\nii.\n```python\nBAD_QUALITY = {'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}\nkeep_clu[prb] = [i for i, qq in enumerate(q) if qq.lower() not in BAD_QUALITY]\n# ...\nfr = mean_firing_rates(obj, probes, keep_clu, gocue, ntrials)\nuse = fr > LOW_FR  # LOW_FR = 1.0\n```\n\niii. The quality label set matches `findClusters.m` exactly. The 1 Hz threshold matches `removeLowFRClusters.m` and the paper statement \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included.\"\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Spike times are aligned to the go cue by subtracting `bp.ev.goCue[trial]` from `clu.trialtm`. This follows `alignSpikes.m`: `trialtm_aligned = trialtm - ev.(alignEvent)(trial)`.\n\nii.\n```python\nal = tt - gocue[tr - 1]  # trialtm_aligned\n```\n\niii. This is a direct port of the reference alignment code, with `alignEvent = 'goCue'`.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. 10 ms bins (DT = 0.01), yielding 500 timepoints per trial over [-2.5, 2.5] s. No rebinning is applied — this is the native bin size used throughout.\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 0.01  # params.dt = 1/100 s  -> 10 ms bins\nEDGES = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT / 2, DT)\n```\n\niii. The AI cites `params.dt = 1/100` from the reference MATLAB code. The AI notes that 5 ms is mentioned in a tutorial comment and that the reference decoding analyses re-bin to 75 ms, but chose 10 ms as the canonical parameter value.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Not derived from raw data variables per se — it is the bin centers of the time axis, computed from the window parameters and bin size.\n\nii.\n```python\nEDGES, TAXIS = time_axis()\n# TAXIS = edges + DT/2, from -2.495 to 2.495 s in 10 ms steps\ninp = TAXIS.astype(np.float32)[None, :]\n```\n\niii. The time axis is defined by the analysis parameters (alignment, window, bin size) and is the same for every trial and session.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds*?\n\ni. Bin centers are computed as `edges + dt/2`, following `getSeq.m`'s `obj.time = edges + dt/2`. No other processing.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef time_axis():\n    edges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT / 2, DT)\n    edges = edges[edges <= TMAX + 1e-9]\n    t = edges + DT / 2\n    return edges, t[:-1]\n```\n\niii. Follows the reference's time axis definition.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The input IS the time axis of the neural binning grid. Spikes are binned into `EDGES`, and the input is the center of each bin. They share the same time axis by construction.\n\nii.\n```python\nb = np.floor((al - TMIN) / DT).astype(np.int64)  # spike -> bin index\n# ...\ninp = TAXIS.astype(np.float32)[None, :]           # same time axis\n```\n\niii. Both are defined by the same `EDGES`/`TAXIS` arrays.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Three per-trial fields: `bp.R` (right-instructed), `bp.L` (left-instructed), `bp.hit` (correct response), `bp.miss` (incorrect response), and `bp.no` (ignore/no response).\n\nii.\n```python\nhit, miss, no = obj.bp('hit'), obj.bp('miss'), obj.bp('no')\nR, L = obj.bp('R'), obj.bp('L')\n```\n\niii. The lick direction is not directly recorded but is derived from the combination of instructed side and outcome.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction*?\n\ni. Hit + right-instructed = right lick; hit + left-instructed = left lick; miss + right-instructed = left lick (wrong side); miss + left-instructed = right lick (wrong side); no-response = none. Codes: left=0, right=1, none=2.\n\nii.\n```python\nlickdir = np.full(ntrials, 2, dtype=np.int8)                 # none\nlickdir[((R > 0.5) & (hit > 0.5)) | ((L > 0.5) & (miss > 0.5))] = 1   # right\nlickdir[((L > 0.5) & (hit > 0.5)) | ((R > 0.5) & (miss > 0.5))] = 0   # left\nlickdir[no > 0.5] = 2\n```\n\niii. This logic follows `getPrevChoice.m` which defines choice as `(R&hit) | (L&miss)` for right. The AI verified this against actual lickport contacts and found 100% agreement (within the response window).\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Behavioral context* derived from?\n\ni. `bp.autowater` — a per-trial flag that is 1 for water-cued (WC) trials and 0 for delayed-response (DR) trials.\n\nii.\n```python\nautowater = obj.bp('autowater')\n```\n\niii. The reference code uses `autowater` as the WC proxy throughout.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Behavioral context*?\n\ni. Direct relabelling: autowater > 0.5 → WC (0), otherwise → DR (1).\n\nii.\n```python\ncontext = np.where(autowater > 0.5, 0, 1).astype(np.int8)  # 0=WC, 1=DR\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder task specification (WC, DR).\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Three per-trial flags: `bp.hit`, `bp.miss`, and `bp.no`. All three are used explicitly.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit, miss, no = obj.bp('hit'), obj.bp('miss'), obj.bp('no')\n```\n\niii. The three flags are mutually exclusive and exhaustive.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. Direct mapping: miss → incorrect (0), hit → correct (1), no → ignore (2).\n\nii.\n```python\noutcome = np.full(ntrials, -1, dtype=np.int8)\noutcome[miss > 0.5] = 0   # incorrect\noutcome[hit > 0.5] = 1    # correct\noutcome[no > 0.5] = 2     # ignore\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder task specification. A runtime check ensures no trial is left with -1 (unlabelled).\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue velocity* derived from?\n\ni. The DeepLabCut tracking in `obj.traj`, specifically the side camera (view 0), feature `'tongue'`. The x,y coordinates and frame times are extracted per trial. `bp.ev.goCue` and `sglx.bitcode.bitstart`/`sglx.fs`/`bp.ev.bitStart` are used for clock alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\nTONGUE_VIEW, TONGUE_FEAT = 0, 'tongue'\n# ...\ntongue_speed, tongue_vis = kinematic_speed(obj, TONGUE_VIEW, TONGUE_FEAT,\n                                           gocue, ntrials, vidshift)\n```\n\niii. The AI uses only the side camera for tongue, consistent with the reference `findPosition.m` which processes features from their native camera view.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. (1) DLC x,y positions are interpolated onto the neural time axis using `interp1` semantics (NaN outside data range). (2) Speed is computed as `sqrt(gradient(x)^2 + gradient(y)^2)` using a NaN-aware gradient. (3) Visibility is determined by whether both x and y are finite after interpolation. (4) The speed is split at the per-session 50th percentile of valid samples. (5) Invisible timepoints get class 2.\n\nii.\n```python\nx = interp_matlab(taxis, tv, xy[0])\ny = interp_matlab(taxis, tv, xy[1])\nvis = np.isfinite(x) & np.isfinite(y)\nvx = nan_gradient(x)\nvy = nan_gradient(y)\nspeed[t] = np.sqrt(vx ** 2 + vy ** 2)\nvisible[t] = vis & np.isfinite(speed[t])\n# ...\ntng_d, tng_thr = discretize(tongue_speed[keep], tongue_vis[keep])\n```\n\niii. The interpolation approach follows `findPosition.m` which interpolates DLC traces onto the neural time axis. The velocity computation follows `findVelocity.m`. The discretization follows the decoder task specification.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session 50th percentile (median) of all valid (visible) tongue speed samples from retained trials. Below median = 0, at or above median = 1, not visible = 2.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize(values, valid, thresh=None):\n    v = np.full(values.shape, 2, dtype=np.int8)\n    if thresh is None:\n        thresh = np.nanpercentile(values[valid], 50) if valid.any() else np.nan\n    if valid.any():\n        v[valid] = (values[valid] >= thresh).astype(np.int8)\n    return v, thresh\n```\n\niii. Follows the decoder task specification exactly (50th percentile, per-session).\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Frame times are corrected from the video clock to the behavior/ephys clock using `findVideoOffset`: `vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs - mode(bp.ev.bitStart)`. Aligned frame time = `frameTimes - vidshift - goCue[trial]`. The DLC position is then interpolated onto the neural time axis (`TAXIS`), so the velocity shares the same time grid.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef video_offset(obj):\n    bitstart = obj.sglx_bitstart()\n    fs = obj.sglx_fs()\n    bp_bitstart = obj.bp('ev.bitStart')\n    return (stats.mode(bitstart, keepdims=False).mode / fs\n            - stats.mode(bp_bitstart, keepdims=False).mode)\n\ntv = ft - vidshift - gocue[t]\nx = interp_matlab(taxis, tv, xy[0])\n```\n\niii. This is a direct port of `findVideoOffset.m` and `findPosition.m`.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Paw velocity* derived from?\n\ni. The DLC tracking from the bottom camera (view 1), feature `'top_paw'`.\n\nii.\n```python\nPAW_VIEW, PAW_FEAT = 1, 'top_paw'\npaw_speed, paw_vis = kinematic_speed(obj, PAW_VIEW, PAW_FEAT,\n                                     gocue, ntrials, vidshift)\n```\n\niii. `top_paw` from the bottom camera is the feature used in the reference's Figure 1e (`top_paw_yvel_view2`).\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Paw velocity*?\n\ni. Same pipeline as tongue velocity: interpolate x,y onto neural time axis, compute speed as magnitude of NaN-aware gradient, split at session median.\n\nii.\n```python\npaw_speed, paw_vis = kinematic_speed(obj, PAW_VIEW, PAW_FEAT, ...)\npaw_d, paw_thr = discretize(paw_speed[keep], paw_vis[keep])\n```\n\niii. Same `kinematic_speed` function is used for both tongue and paw.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Paw velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Same as tongue: per-session 50th percentile of valid samples. 0 = below, 1 = at or above, 2 = not visible.\n\nii.\n```python\npaw_d, paw_thr = discretize(paw_speed[keep], paw_vis[keep])\n```\n\niii. Follows the decoder task specification.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Paw velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same alignment as tongue: video offset correction, go cue subtraction, interpolation onto the neural time axis.\n\nii.\n```python\ntv = ft - vidshift - gocue[t]\nx = interp_matlab(taxis, tv, xy[0])\n```\n\niii. Same `kinematic_speed` function handles alignment for all DLC features.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Motion energy* derived from?\n\ni. A separate file `motionEnergy_<anm>_<date>.mat` containing per-trial traces at 400 Hz (one value per camera frame). Three layouts are handled (bare cell array, `me.data` cell, `me.data.data` struct).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_motion_energy(path):\n    d = sio.loadmat(path, struct_as_record=False, squeeze_me=False)\n    me = d['me']\n    # handles three layouts...\n    return [np.asarray(x, float).ravel() for x in cells.ravel()]\n```\n\niii. Mirrors `loadMotionEnergy.m` which handles the same layout variations (`if isstruct(me.data), me.data = me.data.data`).\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Motion energy*?\n\ni. The raw motion energy trace (already one scalar per frame) is interpolated onto the neural time axis using the video-corrected frame times. No additional smoothing or spatial processing. Split at the session 50th percentile.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef motion_energy_trace(obj, me_cells, gocue, ntrials, vidshift):\n    for t in range(min(ntrials, len(me_cells))):\n        ft = obj.frame_times(0, t)\n        # fallback to 400 Hz nominal clock if needed\n        out[t] = interp_matlab(taxis, ft - vidshift - gocue[t], m)\n    return out\n```\n\niii. The spatial reduction to one value per frame was done upstream by the paper's pipeline. The AI follows `loadMotionEnergy.m` for the interpolation.\n\n## 9-c. Per-session 50th percentile of valid (finite) motion energy samples. 0 = below, 1 = at or above, 2 = no video.\n\nii.\n```python\nme_d, me_thr = discretize(me[keep], me_vis[keep])\n```\n\niii. Follows the decoder task specification.\n\n## 9-d. How is `output` *Motion energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same as tongue/paw: video offset + go cue subtraction on frame times, then interpolation onto the neural time axis. Motion energy uses the side camera frame times.\n\nii.\n```python\nft = obj.frame_times(0, t)  # side camera\nout[t] = interp_matlab(taxis, ft - vidshift - gocue[t], m)\n```\n\niii. Same alignment pipeline as the DLC features, following `loadMotionEnergy.m`.\n\n## 10. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases: (1) Trials where `NdroppedFrames` is NaN are skipped entirely (no video data used) — following `findPosition.m`. (2) Where `frameTimes` is missing or not all finite, a fallback 400 Hz nominal clock is used. (3) DLC positions that are NaN (untracked) propagate through interpolation, resulting in NaN speed that becomes the \"not visible\" class. (4) Trials with no spikes at all are excluded via the no-ephys filter. (5) Motion energy files with fewer trials than `Ntrials` are handled by `min(ntrials, len(me_cells))`.\n\nii.\n```python\nif xy is None or not np.isfinite(obj.ndropped(view, t)):\n    continue                                   # no usable video\nif ft is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(ft)) or ft.size != xy.shape[1]:\n    ft = np.arange(1, xy.shape[1] + 1) / 400.0 - 0.5 + vidshift  # fallback\n```\n\niii. The handling follows the reference MATLAB code patterns (e.g., `findPosition.m` skips trials with `isnan(NdroppedFrames)`).\n\n## 11-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. Video interpolation (DLC kinematics + motion energy) takes ~2.3 s per session, dominating the ~4 s per-session processing time. The full 44-session conversion runs in ~107 s. File loading and spike binning are secondary.\n\nii.\n```python\ninfo['timing'] = {k: round(v, 2) for k, v in timing.items()}\n# timing breakdown: curation, neural, video, total\n```\n\niii. The per-unit spike binning uses `np.bincount` on a flattened index, which is already vectorized across trials.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor t in range(ntrials):\n    xy, ft = obj.traj_xy(view, t, featix)\n    # ... per-trial interpolation\n```\n\niii.
